<note>Original text: "Hiſt. of the Bible". The letter "ſ" seen in the original is a <term>long s</term>, a form of the letter 's' that was common in printing and handwriting until the early 19th century. The word "Hist." is a standard abbreviation for "History."</note>

<note>The volume is bound in a style known as "tree marble." This decorative pattern was created by tilting the book cover and allowing acidic liquids to flow down the surface, creating branching, vein-like designs that resemble trees or marble. The title is gold-stamped onto a red leather label affixed to the spine.</note>

<summary>The front cover and spine of a historical volume titled "History of the Bible," featuring a red leather title label and decorative tree-marbled paper boards.</summary>

### Microcosmic
# Preludes
->Of the<-
## New Heaven and the New Earth;

How man—as the image of **GOD**—is permitted by **GOD** to bring forth from the old, cursed earth a new earth blessed by heaven, in a microcosmic <term>microcosmic</term> <note>From the idea that the human being is a "little world" that reflects the "macrocosm" or the entire universe.</note> and quintessential <term>quintessential</term> <note>Relating to the "fifth essence," the purest and most concentrated spiritual part of a substance, often sought in alchemy.</note> manner, for the delight of the soul and the preservation of the body:
<margin><insert>B = 9</insert></margin>
->Likewise,<-
The nature of Paradise and the Fall of **A**dam, and how **JE**sus can and will restore the entire creation currently lying under the curse and make it paradisiacal again; and how, for this reason, He has made a beginning in His own body, which He took on from **M**ary,
<margin><insert>B = 12</insert></margin>
Presented to the eyes of the world for the glorification of the great **J**ehovah <note>original: "Jehovah"; the Hebrew name of God.</note>, according to the two eternal invisible principles <term>principles</term> <note>original: "principis"; fundamental spiritual forces or origins from which all things arise.</note> working within outer visible nature, namely Light and Darkness,
->by<-
### A Lover of Divine and Natural Mysteries.

---

->Amsterdam, In the Year 1733.<-

<summary>This title page introduces a mystical and alchemical treatise focused on the spiritual restoration of the world and the human body through divine principles. It frames the work as an exploration of how the "cursed" material world can be returned to its "paradisiacal" state, following the example of Jesus.</summary>
<keywords>microcosm, quintessence, alchemy, restoration of creation, Fall of Adam, Light and Darkness, Jehovah, 18th-century mysticism</keywords>

# Preface.

<margin>Nature of things</margin>
There is but one single thing in the **nature of things** <note>original Latin: *rerum natura*</note>; whoever possesses this, possesses everything that is in heaven and on earth; but whoever does not have it, has nothing, even if he were to possess everything else. This single thing is a <term>Philosophical Child <note>An alchemical metaphor for the "Philosopher's Stone" or the perfected substance that results from the "Great Work."</note></term>, which is fathered by God and born through Nature. Because, however, in its first birth it became externally very stained and impure through corrupted nature, it therefore requires a <term>rebirth <note>Alchemists often compared the chemical purification of matter to the spiritual rebirth described in Christian theology.</note></term> from <term>Philosophical Water <note>Refers to a transformative solvent or "mercurial" liquid used to dissolve and purify the base material in the alchemist's vessel.</note></term> and spirit; it also [must transform] itself in such

<summary>The author introduces the concept of a single, universal substance called the "Philosophical Child," which represents the ultimate goal of alchemy and must undergo a process of purification and rebirth.</summary>
<keywords>alchemy, nature of things, Philosophical Child, rebirth, Philosophical Water, purification, Great Work</keywords>

--- Page 11 ---
<meta>This page continues the Preface from the previous leaf. The text follows the discussion of the "Philosophical Child," picking up from the previous page's final sentence: "it also [must transform] itself in such [a state]."</meta>

state that Nature is completely unable to achieve on its own; therefore, during its **rebirth** <note>In alchemical thought, the "rebirth" refers to the purification of matter after it has been broken down to its primal state.</note>, the **Philosopher** <note>original: *Philosophus*; here referring to the practicing alchemist.</note> must perform the duties of a **midwife**, if it is to come into the world successfully. When this same Child is finally born, it grants to the one who assisted it all heavenly and earthly delight in this world.

Many thousands of books have been written about this Child—concerning how and where it may be obtained—but very few of them are genuine and true. Among the ancients, who were true **Adepts** <note>original: *Adepti*; those who have mastered the "Great Work" of alchemy.</note> of the **Philosophical Child**, one mostly finds the greatest **sophistries** <note>original: *Sophistereyen*; deliberately obscure or misleading language used to veil secrets from the uninitiated.</note>, which has been attributed to these good people for many hundreds of years as a sign of their "envy" <note>The "Envy of the Philosophers" (*invidia philosophorum*) was a common trope claiming masters hid the truth to prevent the unworthy from gaining power.</note>. 

However, it must be understood that they have been done an injustice in this regard; they did not write in such a way out of envy, but for highly significant reasons, so that **pearls would not be cast before swine** <note>A reference to Matthew 7:6, a common justification for alchemical secrecy.</note>. He who has open eyes will find the fruit beneath such leaves <note>The author uses a wordplay on "leaves" (*Blätter*), referring both to the leaves of a tree hiding fruit and the pages of a book hiding the truth.</note>, and among the

<summary>The author explains that the alchemist acts as a "midwife" to nature to produce the Philosophical Child and defends ancient alchemists, arguing their confusing writing was not due to envy but a necessary measure to protect sacred knowledge.</summary>

<keywords>alchemy, rebirth, midwife metaphor, Philosophical Child, Adepts, sophistries, Envy of the Philosophers, pearls before swine, secrecy</keywords>

...well see the roses among the thorns.
Others have written using nothing but *hieroglyphics* <note>original: *Hieroglyphicis*; symbolic or cryptic images used to hide alchemical secrets from the uninitiated.</note> and figures without providing actual *processes*, in order to lead the seekers of this divine *being* <note>original: *Entis*; referring here to the "Divine Essence" or the Philosopher's Stone.</note> into the depths of God's creation, just as *Ovid* did in his *Metamorphoses* <note>Ovid's Roman myths of transformation were often interpreted by alchemists as coded chemical instructions.</note>, etc. Still others write of the four elements and their *transmutation* <note>The conversion of one substance or element into another.</note> of one into the other, as well as of the three *principles* <note>original: *Principiis*; in alchemy, these are typically Mercury, Sulfur, and Salt, the basic components of all matter.</note>, explaining how they must be purified from their *earthiness* <note>original: *Terrestrität*; the crude, solid impurities of a substance before it is refined.</note> through proper *solution* <note>The process of dissolving a solid into a liquid state to purify it.</note> and then unified through the action of the natural fire. These are the best writers, whom I have followed.

There is also a type of *writer* <note>original: *Scribenten*; used here somewhat dismissively for unoriginal or deceptive authors.</note> who either creates *collections* <note>original: *Collectanea*; anthologies or compilations gathered from various sources.</note> from various *philosophers* and mixes their "mouse-droppings"—that is, their *chimeras* <note>original: *Chimæren*; wild, unfounded fantasies or false ideas.</note> and [false] *processes*—into the "philosophical pepper" <note>A colorful idiom suggesting they are mixing worthless filth into valuable, spicy knowledge.</note>; or else, in their blindness, they seek to interpret this or that true *author* <note>original: *Auctorem*.</note> who has written from the light of God and Nature, just to acquire the title of an *Adept* <note>original: *Adepti*; one who has mastered the "Great Work" of alchemy.</note>, and under such a name, to deceive the ignorant...

<summary>The author categorizes different types of alchemical writers, distinguishing between those who use allegory (like Ovid), those who focus on the three principles and four elements, and "scribblers" who compile false information to deceive others.</summary>

[...deceive] <note>This completes the word from the previous page.</note>, against which one must most carefully guard oneself, and call upon **God** for His light, so that one is not led astray. It is the same with the interpretation of *philosophical writings* <note>In this context, "philosophical" specifically refers to alchemical texts; alchemists often called themselves "philosophers of nature."</note> as it is with the interpretation of the Bible. Whoever wishes to understand and interpret these must unfailingly possess the *spirit* <note>original: *Geist*</note> from which and in which they were written; and whoever wishes to understand the *philosophers* <note>original: *Philosophos*; here referring to the true masters of alchemy.</note> must possess the very same *light* <note>original: *Licht*; referring to divine or intellectual illumination required to see the "truth" behind cryptic alchemical allegories.</note> which shone upon those philosophers. Where this is absent, he will *reason* <note>original: *raisonniren*; to argue or intellectualize.</note> about their writings just as a blind man reasons about color.

He can speak of great fortune whom **God** preserves from *sophistry* <note>original: *Sophisterey*; here meaning deceptive, overly complex, or false alchemical theories that sound plausible but lead to failure.</note> and from books of that kind, or to whom He grants a friend who warns him and points him toward the right path. deceivers. For even though they often mix in grand truths from the <term>Hermetic writings</term> <note>original: *Hermeticis*; referring to the works attributed to Hermes Trismegistus or the body of alchemical literature</note>, these truths are not valid in their hands, because they have so stained and darkened them with their errors that the readers of such <term>worthless pamphlets</term> <note>original: *Chartequen*; a derogatory term for cheap books, scribblings, or legalistic paperwork</note>, who might otherwise have possessed a little natural light, are completely blinded by them. It is a very certain <term>mark</term> <note>original: *Marque*</note> that he who borrows from others has nothing of his own. He who understands the matter has no need to borrow; rather, Nature dictates more to him than he can write.

As I once turned my mind toward <term>Hermetic Philosophy</term> <note>The study of alchemy and the transformation of matter and spirit</note> and obtained such books that seemed to make the profound speeches of the philosophers entirely clear through <term>recipes</term> <note>original: *Recepte*; specific alchemical formulas</note> and <term>processes</term> <note>original: *Processe*; laboratory procedures</note>, I thought I already had the fish in the <term>landing net</term> <note>original: *Hamen*; a bag-shaped fishing net</note>, though I was not even yet by the water in which one must fish. ...alone what I have entreated from GOD and learned through deep reflection from certain philosophical books; for all those whom I perhaps came to know knew nothing themselves and were drowned in <term>sophistry</term> <note>In an alchemical context, "sophistry" refers to the practice of "false" alchemy—using deceptive chemical processes or common materials that do not lead to the true Stone.</note>.

Phil.*, short for *Lapis Philosophorum*, the legendary substance capable of transmuting base metals into gold and granting long life.</note> to follow, so that they may save their money and their labor, because they can obtain their desire for free from Nature, to whom they must only provide a small <term>assistance</term> <note>original: *Subministration*; the alchemical belief that the artist does not "create" the work, but merely provides the right conditions for Nature to perform its own transmutations.</note> in her operation. Among those books that I have read, there are a few that are clean of sophistry, namely Sendivogius, Bernhardus, the Farmer, and the <term>Hermetic Triumph</term> <note>original: *Hermetischen Triumphic.*, likely referring to the 1689 book *Le Triomphe Hermétique*, a famous alchemical dialogue.</note>. The others that I encountered were either only <term>speculative philosophers</term> <note>original: *Speculativi Philosophi*; scholars who write from theory and logic rather than from successful practical experience in the laboratory.</note> or were very much obscured by sophistications.

--- Page 20 ---
<meta>This page continues the critique of the writings attributed to the legendary alchemist Basil Valentine. It explains why his works are often confusing—blaming posthumous publishers for mixing genuine and forged texts—and distinguishes between common medicinal chemistry and the "True Tincture" of the Philosopher's Stone.</meta>

...elaborated in his first years, so mixed up that hardly anyone can make sense of them; and this is because, out of ignorance after his death (for during his life he published nothing), all his writings—the true along with the false—were gathered together indiscriminately <note>original: *promiscue*</note> and shared with the public, to the great darkening of the <term>Light of Nature</term> <note>original: *Lichts der Natur*; a concept popularized by Paracelsus referring to the divine wisdom inherent in the natural world which guides the physician and alchemist.</note>. 

His *Twelve Keys* and his *Mountain-Book* <note>original: *12. Schlüſſel* and *Berg-Buch*; two of the most famous works attributed to Basil Valentine, a supposedly 15th-century monk whose writings actually began appearing in the early 1600s.</note> are without doubt the best and most sincere; but because he believed he had revealed the truth all too clearly in the former, he himself somewhat obscured it in the so-called *Explanation of the Twelve Keys* <note>original: *Erklärüng der 12. Schlüſſel*</note> for the sake of the unworthy—just as most people fall for such an "Explanation" and thus follow a side-path <note>In alchemy, a "side-path" refers to a secondary or incorrect chemical process that might produce interesting results but fails to achieve the Philosopher's Stone.</note>. 

It cannot be denied that fine <term>medicine</term> <note>original: *Medicin*; here referring to chemical remedies or "spagyric" preparations meant for healing the human body.</note> can be found here and there in his processes; however, that is far removed from a <term>Tincture</term> <note>original: *Tinctur*; the highest goal of alchemy, the "Great Work" capable of transmuting base metals into gold and healing all disease.</note>, which requires a completely different <term>matter</term> <note>original: *Materie*; the primary substance or "First Matter" used to begin the alchemical work.</note> unknown to the world, and a completely different fire and operation.

<summary>The author warns that many works attributed to Basil Valentine are posthumous forgeries or "obscured" by the author himself to hide secrets from the unworthy. He distinguishes between Valentine's useful medicinal recipes and the much more difficult secret of the True Tincture.</summary>
<keywords>Basil Valentine, Twelve Keys, Mountain-Book, Light of Nature, alchemy, Tincture, First Matter, posthumous publications</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the discussion on the "True Tincture" by contrasting common laboratory chemistry with the spiritual and philosophical depths of the Hermetic art. The previous page concluded by noting that while Basil Valentine's works contain useful medical processes, they are often distinct from the true goal of the Philosopher's Stone.</meta>

Common chemistry <term>boils</term> <note>original: *kochet*; in alchemy, "cooking" or "boiling" refers to the application of heat to a substance to cause maturation or transformation.</note> water and other matters in fire, but the **Hermetic Philosophers** <note>original: *Hermetici Philosophi*; followers of the legendary Hermes Trismegistus who seek the ultimate secrets of nature and spirit.</note> boil fire in water. This is the difference and the entire secret, which consists in nothing other than **dissolving** <note>original: *solviren*</note> and **coagulating** <note>original: *coaguliren*; the foundational alchemical maxim *Solve et Coagula* refers to the process of breaking a substance down to its core and then reforming it into a higher, purer state.</note>. He who can grasp it, let him grasp it.

If it were not against my **intention** to be verbose, I could have greatly **extended** this little treatise without borrowing anything from others; however, I would have only increased the confusion in this Divine **Science** <note>original: *Scienz*</note> rather than diminishing it. Therefore, I have **concentrated** all verbosity in order to place the righteous lovers of divine and natural secrets, through the reading of this work, with their minds into the **Center**, from which they can oversee the entire **Periphery** <note>In Hermetic thought, the "Center" represents the source of all things (God or the Prima Materia), while the "Periphery" represents the manifested world of diversity.</note>. 

And certainly, he who does not fundamentally understand the general generation and destruction of things, which proceeds from the **Center**, let him not venture into the **Hermetic**—

<summary>The author distinguishes "common chemistry" from the "Hermetic" art by using the paradox of "boiling fire in water" and emphasizes that his short treatise is designed to lead the reader directly to the central principles of nature.</summary>

### §. 1.

The Highest Good <note>original: *Summum Bonum*</note> or the highest happiness in this world, which all people seek and desire—if not openly, then secretly—consists of a complete contentment of the mind. This contentment of the mind, however, if it is to exist or be present, requires 1) a healthy body. 2) That one does not depend <term>dependire: to be reliant upon or subservient to others</term> on anyone, whether high or low, for their livelihood <term>Subsistenz: the means of supporting one's life and basic needs</term> and physical nourishment. 3) That one is assured of the grace of God through an essential knowledge <term>Erkantniß: a deep, spiritual understanding or recognition beyond mere intellectual learning</term> and internal feeling, and thus possesses a quiet conscience and a constant joy toward Him. Whoever lacks one of these three parts is still far removed from complete contentment of the mind, and therefore from the highest happiness.

§. 2. A healthy body is a noble gift from God, but if temporal nourishment and a quiet conscience are lacking, then a person can never become truly content within themselves.

§. 3. Possessing temporal goods in the world brings great prestige, and [they] are in almost everyone's eyes—

<meta>This page marks the beginning of the primary text following the author's preface dated November 1732. The author shifts from introductory apologies to a philosophical and theological definition of human happiness.</meta>

<image-desc>A decorative woodcut headpiece featuring a large central flower resembling a sunflower or stylized rose, flanked by symmetrical scrolling acanthus leaves and smaller floral blossoms.</image-desc>

### §. 1.

The *Highest Good* <note>original: *Summum Bonum*; a Latin philosophical term referring to the ultimate importance or the ultimate goal of human existence</note> or the highest happiness in this world, which all people seek and desire—if not publicly, then secretly—consists of a complete contentment of the mind. This contentment of the mind, however, if it is to *exist* or be, requires: 
1) A healthy body. 
2) That one does not *depend* on anyone, whether high or low, for their *subsistence* <term>subsistence: the means of supporting life, such as food and basic needs</term> and physical nourishment. 
3) That one is assured of the grace of GOD through an essential knowledge and internal feeling, and thus has a quiet conscience and a constant joy toward Him. 
Whoever lacks one of these three pieces is still far removed from complete contentment of the mind, and thus from the highest happiness.

§. 2: A healthy body is a noble gift from GOD, but if temporal nourishment and a quiet conscience are lacking, then a person can never become satisfied within themselves.

§. 3: To possess temporal goods in the world brings great prestige, and they are [the focus] of almost all eyes—

<summary>The author begins his treatise by defining the "Highest Good" as complete contentment of the mind, which he argues relies on three pillars: physical health, financial independence, and a clear conscience through God's grace.</summary>

<keywords>Highest Good, happiness, contentment, health, independence, divine grace, conscience, subsistence</keywords>

and hearts set upon them. But when one considers how they are acquired nowadays, and that for their attainment not only health but often the conscience must be *postponed* <note>original: *postponiret*; meaning to set aside or treat as secondary to one's goals</note>, then once again, regarding the contentment of the mind, a great *defect* <note>original: *Defect*; a lack or deficiency</note> is present.

§. 4. Some, though very few, indeed have a quiet conscience and therefore a joy toward God; but because they are often besieged either by the unhealthiness of the body or by a lack of nourishment, their contentment of mind is frequently interrupted and disturbed.

§. 5. Now the question is: how should one begin so that one may perfectly possess the above-mentioned three pieces in this world without harm to another and with a good conscience—and specifically in such a way that one loses nothing of the Eternal and the future, but rather gains? Answer: whoever wishes to attain this more than royal happiness must also have more than royal contemplations and be of a more than royal mind. He must seek with his whole heart the Wisdom <note>The author likely refers to *Sophia* or Divine Wisdom, a central figure in Christian mysticism</note> that always hovers around the fiery throne of God and dwells both in eternal Nature and in all creatures. He must choose her for his light and his companion; he must *consider* <note>original: *consideriren*; to contemplate or examine closely</note> her creations—what and how they were before they came to be, and through what mediation they were born from the innermost into the outermost, or from eternity into time. *Also* <note>original: *Item*; a Latin term used to introduce a new point or item in a list</note>, what God's and Nature's right

§. 6. All these *General Positions* <note>original: *General-Positiones*; fundamental principles or theses</note> will give the lover of divine and natural wisdom sufficient instruction to recognize upon what the *Highest Good* <note>original: *Summum bonum*; the supreme principle of perfection</note> of Nature is founded; and when he cleanses himself of all defilement of the flesh and the spirit, then Wisdom will meet him on the way, behave kindly toward him, and gradually show and offer him all heavenly and earthly treasures for health and wealth in this life, and for unending joy in the life to come. Indeed, she will kindle a light within him, by which he will see himself alongside all *creatures* <note>original: *Creaturen*; all created beings</note> in his origin, namely, in GOD, and in turn see GOD in himself and in all *creatures*, which can then produce nothing other than a perfect contentment of the mind.

§. 7. And since they have heard and read that people are found in the world who, through a divine and natural art, can not only constantly protect themselves from sickness and poverty, but also stand in great joy of the spirit and in perfect satisfaction of their souls through the contemplation of the glories of GOD, such inestimable things indeed please them very well, because

<margin>They do not seek within Nature</margin>

§. 8. Just as a natural raven cannot give birth to a swan, so too these "bats" <note>A metaphor for those who are blind to the light of truth</note> with their unnatural subjects <note>original: *Subjectis*; the raw materials used in an experiment</note> and labors <note>original: *Laboribus*</note> cannot bring the philosophical Sun- and Moon-child <note>original: *Sonnen- und Monden-Kind*; a symbolic name for the Philosopher's Stone, representing the union of opposites</note> into the world. They seek the divine and natural art without the knowledge <note>original: *Erkäntniß*</note> of God and Nature; indeed, they do not even know what kind of thing Nature is, much less how, with what, and where she works. They constantly wander in their minds around the periphery <note>The outer edges or superficial aspects of a subject</note> and know how to speak of many effects <note>original: *Effectibus*</note> of Nature, but they do not find the center of nature <note>original: *Centrum naturæ*</note> which causes <note>original: *causiret*</note> all effects. Where they should be extracting the seed <note>original: *Saamen*</note> from the living metals in the living fire, and thus making <term>mercury through mercury</term> <note>original: *Mercurium per Mercurium*</note>, or <term>prime matter through prime matter</term> <note>original: *Materiam primam per Materiam primam*; the fundamental, original substance from which all things are created</note>, they do not even know what the life and the seed of the metals are—much less what mercury <note>original: *Mercurius*</note> or <term>prime matter</term> <note>original: *Materia prima*</note> might be. Instead, they toil with dead and expired subjects <note>original: *Subjectis*</note>, such as common gold, silver, mercury, etc., and

§. 22. Those who have no thorough knowledge of Creation and do not understand the essential language of God believe that the Holy Trinity *deliberated* <note>original: *deliberiret*</note> and spoke with one another outside of Nature and the creatures: "Let us make man, an image that is like us." This fleshly *conception* <note>original: *Concept*</note> hinders us, so that in our lack of self-knowledge we understand neither the language of Moses nor that of God. Although no one will or can deny the divine *Trinity* <note>original: *Trinitatem*</note>, the wise Moses intends something quite different here with the little word "Us"—namely, to say exactly what I have just mentioned regarding the *quintessence* of the whole *Creation*. principally on the divine Trinity outside of Nature and creation. Why then does one deny the third part in the creation of man, and even maintain—contrary to the clearest passages of Holy Scripture—that there are generally only two

[understoo]d, and has no name, Exodus 3:14. Therefore He is only a Spirit, John 4:24.

§. 24. However, when the One **GOD** desired to reveal Himself in creatures, and for this reason emerged from His innermost hiddenness and took on a somewhat more comprehensible essence—and also, before the smaller creatures could appear, first had to undertake a separation of such essence—one has since found a trinity everywhere through the thorough investigation and anatomy of creatures. From this, one must argue that the <term>Effect</term> <note>original Latin: *Effectus*; the result or creation</note> cannot testify to anything other than its <term>Cause</term> <note>original Latin: *Causa*; the source or creator</note>, and thus there must necessarily be a Triune **GOD** who has birthed such trinities out of Himself.

§. 25. The passage in Genesis 3:22, where <term>Elohim</term> <note>the Hebrew name for God used in the creation account, which is plural in form</note> spoke in the corrupted external nature: 

> Behold, Adam has become as one of Us, 

has the same meaning as: 

> Let Us make humans. 

But if one wished to interpret such a passage as referring to the Trinity of **GOD** outside of nature and creatures, it would again be asked: after which Person in the Godhead did the then-fallen human take? For it says: "Adam has become as one of Us." From this, horrible <term>absurdities</term> <note>original Latin: *Absurda*; things that are logically impossible or nonsensical</note> and blasphemous conclusions would follow. If the translator of the original text had understood the creation of **GOD**, he would have written here: "Adam has become a unique one out of us (mortal creatures); as-"

And because in the earth the weight of the coarse darkness far exceeds the weight of the concentrated light within the mixture, the artist must separate the abundance of darkness from the light very gently and naturally in the natural furnace, by means of the <term>mercury of the philosophers</term> <note>original: *Mercurii Philosophorum*; a spiritualized essence of mercury, distinct from common quicksilver, used as a universal solvent.</note>, and increase and improve the small light through the great light in **quality** and **quantity** <note>original: *qualitate* and *quantitate*</note> and allow it to thrive unto its perfect ripeness. Whatever it seizes from the light-essences during such condensation, whether **collectively** <note>original: *collectim*</note> or **individually** <note>original: *particulatim*</note>, it simultaneously condenses or concentrates. This is seen in gemstones, which are nothing other than a concentrated light. Through the divine *Fiat* <note>Latin: "Let it be"; the creative command of God.</note>, these were drawn together from purely pure light-particles within the purest **womb** <note>original: *Matrice*; the geological or alchemical environment in which a substance grows.</note> of the earth, and were also made **solid** <note>original: *compact*</note> and **stable** <note>original: *fix*; in alchemy, a "fixed" substance is one that resists the force of fire and does not evaporate.</note> through the central fire.

§. 42. Metals consist of the very same **essence** as gemstones; the only difference between them is that in gemstones—according to the three **principles** <note>The *Tria Prima*, or three primary substances of Paracelsian alchemy: Mercury, Sulfur, and Salt.</note>—**Mercury** <note>original: *Mercurius*</note> predominates, whereas in metals, **Sulfur** <note>original: *Sulphur*</note> **predominates** <note>original: *prædominiret*</note>. For Mercury is fundamentally a pure light, and Sulfur is fundamentally a pure fire. Fire is thick/dense, and light is thin/subtle; therefore, gemstones are transparent and metals are not. The less Sulfur there is in gemstones, the more translucent and clear they are; and the more Sulfur there is in metals, the denser and more stable they are. **Salt** <note>original: *Sal*</note>, however—because it is born from Sulfur and Mercury—is their physical body and **magnet**. It is within the Salt that the first two (Sulfur and Mercury) are contained and become tangible. And depending on whether the **womb** <note>original: *Matrix*</note> or its Salt was pure or impure, pure or impure metals and gemstones were formed.

<meta>catchword: §. 43.</meta>

§. 43. When this light is freed from its dark bonds through the **Sophic Solution** <note>The "Philosophical Solution," a process of dissolving a substance into its primary components without destroying its "seed" or life-force.</note>, it frequently **attracts** and **fixes** the **solar rays** <note>original: *Radios Solares*</note> as its own kind. By this process, the light of the metals is greatly exalted and increased; this is then the true **carbuncle** <note>A legendary gemstone, often red, said to glow in the dark; here it refers to the completed Philosopher's Stone.</note>, which no one is permitted to see except for the **Hermetic Philosophers**. Its very fiery light has received power from the Creator to burn and **calcine** <note>To reduce a mineral to a fine powder through heat.</note> common gold to such an extent that it dissolves like a salt in any **liquid** <note>original: *Liquore*</note> and becomes **potable** <note>original: *potable*; drinkable gold (Aurum Potabile) was considered a universal medicine.</note>, which no other fire in the world is capable of doing.

§. 45. §. 54. If the subtle rays of the sun are to become tangible in the earth, this cannot happen otherwise than by means of air and water. This refers to the alchemical idea that nature "cooks" or digests matter over time to bring it to a state of perfection.</note>. Of these four, there are always two against two; otherwise, there would be no life and no movement. For a simple being <note>A "simple" being here refers to a substance made of only one element or quality, which would be static and unchanging.</note> cannot move, because it is stirred by nothing contrary, but the contrary quality in all things creates a pleasant enmity <note>original: *angenehme Feindschafft*; a paradoxical term for the productive tension between opposing forces (like hot/cold or wet/dry) that fuels the engine of nature.</note> in the whole of nature. Therefore, one element drives another, and in such driving, they promote the births of animals, vegetables, and minerals.

§. 55. Fire and Water are completely contrary to one another, and they have the Air for their union, which **God** composed from a subtler Water and a coarser Fire, so that water might join with water, and fire with fire, and unite magnetically. §. 60. But he must thoroughly understand the four main <term>qualities</term> <note>the traditional Aristotelian qualities: Hot, Cold, Moist, and Dry</note> mentioned above, and apply them to his work; he must operate with elements and through elements, otherwise he will achieve nothing. Because at the beginning of Creation all elements were water, and in their foundation they still are, he must make his beginning with the <term>Philosophical Water</term> <note>a specialized alchemical solvent, often called the "Universal Solvent" or "Mercure des Philosophes," believed to dissolve metals into their primary essence</note>, in which all elements are joined together. For with this, he must make the coarse subtle, and the subtle tangible. That is, he must <term>dissolve</term> <note>original: *solviren*</note> the fire and light that is <term>compacted</term> within the earth—and especially within the <term>metallic workshops</term> <note>the subterranean places where metals were thought to "grow" or mature over time</note>—and with this <term>solution</term>, magnetically draw down and <term>fix</term> <note>to make a volatile substance stable and solid</note> the upper fire and light.

### §. 72.
I turn my philosophical inquiry once more toward Eden, to the single Element <term>single Element: in this mystical tradition, the "pure element" is the spiritual substance from which the four material elements (fire, air, water, earth) originated</term>, which flows from there into external nature like a stream—a stream that once went forth and still constantly flows out, dividing itself there into four main waters, which is to say, into four elements. If this holy stream did not still water and improve the realm of nature every day, everything down here would perish from the dark fierceness <term>fierceness: original: *Grimmigkeit*; a term used in Boehmenist mysticism to describe the harsh, wrathful, or chaotic quality of nature when it is separated from God's light</term>, and nothing would be able to grow. 

Lucifer’s pride provoked this fierceness. For when he desired to be equal to GOD and to become a creator himself, he also required an element for that purpose; and because he initially consisted of fire and light, as that single Element, he took hold of it. However, because he excessively stirred up the contracting *Let it be* <note>original: *Fiat*; the divine creative command</note> in the dark principle <note>original: *Principio*</note> and sought to create outside the order of GOD—and through such compression <note>original: *Compression*; here meaning a spiritual "thickening" or hardening of will</note> blocked the influx of divine light—that light withdrew from him. In the act of creating (as far as his sphere reached), his light degenerated into water, and his fire into earth. 

Then that which was hidden broke forth: for his darkness, which previously had been refined <note>original: *subtilisiret*; an alchemical term for making a substance more spiritual or less dense</note> and illuminated by the light, now became manifest in a dark earth and coarse water, and it gained great power within his circle and domain. ### §. 73.
Now, into everything that the good Creator <term>Creator: original: *Schöpffer*</term> creates here below through His Word and Light, Lucifer mixes in his dark forces, which cause eventual ruin and death to the animal and vegetable kingdoms. ### §. 74.

Through the mediation of this central fire, the waters are now *distilled* through all the *pores* <note>original: *Poros*</note> of the earth up to its *surface* <note>original: *Superficiem*</note>, and even high into the air, as we see in the clouds; and because the rays of this central fire and light marry, as it were, with the rising waters, they partly settle *successively* in the purest and most suitable places of the earth, and the dark earth, as a *most-binding body* <note>original: *constringentissimum corpus*</note>, grasps and *compacts* it. Through this, according to the difference of the locations, various *metals* are born within the earth. Partly, however, it is brought through this *vehicle* <note>original: *Vehiculum*</note> of water even into the *plant kingdom* <note>original: *vegetabilische Reich*</note>, for the enjoyment and preservation of the *animal kingdom*, as the excellent *Sendivogius* the Peasant <note>original: *Sendivogius der Bauer*; likely referring to Michael Sendivogius (1566–1636), a famous Polish alchemist often called "the Pole"</note> and *Basilius Valentinus* <note>a legendary 15th-century alchemist and monk</note> [have written]...

§. 79. As long as metals are in their ores <note>original: *Mineren*</note>, they are a pure <term>Mercury</term> <note>original: *Mercurius*</note> that holds its own <term>Sulfur</term> <note>original: *Sulphur*</note> and <term>Salt</term> <note>original: *Sal*</note> within itself. This Mercury is the bride around which the <term>Philosophers</term> <note>original: *Philosophi*; here meaning alchemists</note> dance. It is the same in all ores, though in one it is more impure and more tightly locked away than in another. In the <term>Ore of Saturn</term> <note>original: *Minera Saturni*; the alchemical name for lead ore</note>, it is at its most soluble.

§. 80. From this Mercury, all metals and minerals are born, for it is their seed, which consists of fire and light. According to the nature and condition of its workshop <note>original: *Werckstadt*; the specific geological environment</note> in the earth, it creates for itself a <term>body</term> <note>original: *Corpus*</note> or a house from its own essence, in which it dwells. Such a body is either gold, silver, copper, or lead, etc., etc.—all according to the condition of the <term>matrix</term> <note>original: *Matrix*; the "womb" or surrounding rock where minerals form</note> or the workshop, and depending on whether it has worked for a long or short time on such a house or body. Now, when an ore is brought into the fire up here <note>referring to the chemist's furnace on the surface</note>, the light-fiery Mercury, as the inhabitant, departs, for it cannot endure the common fire; and the <term>laborant</term> <note>original: *Laborante*; the practical alchemist or worker</note> retains nothing more than a dead body—namely gold, silver, lead, tin, etc., etc. This he then takes, and wishes to reduce it back into a <term>living mercury</term> <note>original: *Mercurium vivum*</note> which, in his opinion, is supposed to do great things.

§. 81. On this path, many millions of people have labored themselves into poverty, sickness, and death, and have found nothing. It seems to me exactly as if someone were to chase an inhabitant out of a house and then wanted to create a new inhabitant out of the house itself; how well would he be following Nature, and how successful would he be? Would not all people consider him insane? It is the same with those who take an <term>ore</term> <note>original Latin: *Minera*</note> and first drive out the living <term>Mercury</term> <note>The alchemical principle of fluidity and life</note> through fire, but then want to create another Mercury out of the dead body. Why then does one not keep the Mercury already prepared by Nature within the ore? If one were to cultivate that, then one would be building upon Nature and not destroying it. 

And although it often happens that laboratory workers, when they <term>vex</term> <note>original: *vexiren*; to subject a substance to harsh or violent chemical processes like intense heat or strong acids</note> common metals too much, occasionally obtain a running [liquid] mercury, it is nonetheless not the <term>Mercury of the Philosophers</term> <note>original Latin: *Mercurius Philosophorum*; the refined, spiritualized essence sought by alchemists, distinct from common quicksilver</note>, and it is of as much use to the <term>Philosophical Work</term> <note>The *Magnum Opus* or Great Work of alchemy</note> as the common kind one buys in a shop. 

The Mercury of the Philosophers is that very pure element which I mentioned above, which remains hidden within the four elements even now after the <term>Fall</term> <note>Referring to the biblical Fall of Man, which alchemists believed "veiled" the true, divine perfection of nature</note>. It is the Paradise planted by God Himself, but hidden and covered because of the Fall, which the <term>Philosopher</term> <note>An alchemist</note> must seek and cultivate, and also move from the house of darkness into the house of light.

§. 83. And because you have become so well-acquainted with GOD, as one friend with another, you will not dread death, which then is a gentle falling asleep without any sickness or pain.

§. 92. Referring to the biblical patriarch Noah, whose warnings of the flood were ignored by his contemporaries.</note>, etc. Since God did not strike immediately the first time and allow the end to come, people now consider these divine heralds to be something entirely natural; they thus separate God and nature, the Creator and the creature, far from one another, though one should rightly consider that God and nature are as closely—indeed, even more closely—united as body and soul, and that God works in no other way than in nature and through nature.

§. 93. Among all the phenomena <term>Phænomena</term> that I have seen, none has appeared greater or stronger to me than the first one; for in the place where I was at that time, it was so bright that I could not only read all manner of writing, but together with others, I could even quite distinctly hear the rising rays as a soft hissing or rushing sound <note>Historical accounts of the aurora borealis often mention these "sounds," which were long debated by scientists but are now understood as related to electromagnetic effects.</note>, and it seemed as if I were in the middle, or at the center <term>Centro</term>, of this appearing light. Though others have told me they observed the same thing in later phenomena, it all depended on whether the eruptions of the central fire <term>centralisches Feuer</term> <note>In theosophical and alchemical thought, the "central fire" was considered an internal, spiritual, or subterranean energy that drove the processes of nature.</note> were large or small in a given location. From this light breaking forth out of the darkness, one can recognize to some extent how a light could appear through God's speaking before the creation of the sun, moon, and stars, as in Genesis 1:3 <note>original: "Gen. I, 3". "And God said, Let there be light: and there was light."</note>.

§. 94. This central fire is of a cold quality, and the astral fire <term>astralisches Feuer</term> <note>The "astral fire" refers to the celestial influences or the energy of the stars and heavens acting upon the earth from above.</note> is of a warm quality. Between these two fires, the greatest mysteries occur, and all plant life <term>Vegetabilia</term> is "cooked" <note>An alchemical term for the process of growth and maturation, where natural forces refine the essence of living things.</note> by them. Both serve God's creation <term>Schöpffung</term>, and the preservation and multiplication of all creatures. The earth, however, is the mother, who through her powerful "Let it be" <term>Fiat</term> <note>Latin for "let it be done," referring to the divine creative command used in Genesis.</note> makes the forces of both fires physical and brings them forth according to the macrocosmic idea <term>macrocosmische Idee</term> <note>The concept that the overarching structure of the universe (the macrocosm) is reflected in the structure of the earth and its inhabitants.</note>. Whatever one of these two creates, the other destroys, and continually makes something new from it once again.

§. 95. If these two contrary qualities did not exist, there would be no corporeality <term>Leiblichkeit</term> <note>The state of having a physical body or material existence.</note>; for through their struggle, the spirits <term>Geister</term> <note>In this context, "spirits" refers to volatile, energetic essences rather than ghosts.</note> grow weary and precipitate <term>præcipitiren</term> <note>original: "præcipitiren"; an alchemical term where a substance falls out of a solution to become a solid.</note>, and they become physical. The warm fire is above, and the cold is below. Although the cold is also in the warm, and the warm in the cold, it is simply that the warm predominates <term>prædominiret</term> above, and the cold below. Likewise, the warm is in the light, and the cold is in the darkness. If you now understand how the spirits become physical through these two fires, you will also learn to see how bodies can become spiritual again; and through such science <term>Scienz</term> you will be successful in your philosophy; for in this knowledge, everything depends on the "making-physical" of the macrocosmic <term>macrocosmisch</term> <note>Relating to the great world or the universe as a whole, as opposed to the microcosm of the individual.</note> spirits.

§. 96. Just as the rays of the warm fire are congealed <term>congeliret</term> by the cold, and the rays of the cold fire are coagulated <term>coaguliret</term> by the warm: so, in turn, the warm fire dissolves the congelation of the cold, and the cold dissolves the coagulation of the warm fire. Regarding this, Basilius Valentinus <note>A legendary 15th-century alchemist, supposedly a Benedictine monk, whose writings were highly influential in the 17th and 18th centuries.</note> philosophizes most magnificently in his entire Mining-Book <note>original: "Berg-Buch"; likely referring to the "Last Testament" or "The Triumphal Chariot of Antimony," which deal with mineralogy and alchemy.</note>, especially in the 48th and 49th chapters. With and through these two primary properties, divine wisdom works and creates all things; and in imitation of this, the philosophers also make their Stone or Tincture <note>The "Philosopher's Stone" or "Tincture" was the legendary substance capable of transmuting base metals into gold and granting eternal life.</note>.

§. 98. For this very reason, it is against the order of God and Nature when the dead are disturbed from their rest in the earth, as when their bones are dug up during the preparation of new graves. These bones are then either left lying in the churchyards <term>Kirch-Höfen</term>, if not entirely then in part, or are even thrown into the bone-houses <term>Bein-Häuser</term> <note>Also known as ossuaries; buildings where skeletal remains were stored when graveyard space became limited.</note> built for that purpose in many places. There they may lie for several hundred years and never reach a state of decomposition <term>Verwesung</term>.

§. 99. In this respect, one who is not buried in the common churchyards is much more blessed than others, for he remains undisturbed in his rest and putrefaction <term>Putrefaktion</term>. If someone were to walk onto our field and pull out our growing grain <term>Geträydig</term> or seeds, and throw them

§. 100. <meta>catchword: §. 103.</meta>

...understood such natural things better. According to the law of nature <term>Recht der Natur</term> that flourished at that time, every head of household <term>Hauß-Vater</term> <note>Literally "House-Father," referring to the patriarch who held legal and spiritual authority over his family and property.</note> could bury his dead either in his field or in his garden, where he believed they could lie most securely. Today, however, since natural law is no longer practiced, but has been replaced by an arbitrary and self-interested law, this freedom has been completely cut off. In every town and every village, there is a small plot of land that bears no proportion to the number of inhabitants. To this place, all the dead have been buried for many hundreds of years; and if the world were to stand for many thousands more years, according to current regulations, everyone dying during that time in that place would have to be brought there. This is just as if someone were to sow several wispels <term>Wispel</term> <note>An old German unit of dry volume used for grain; one wispel could equal roughly 24 to 30 bushels depending on the region.</note> of grain <term>Geträydig</term> in a single field and, before it could even begin to grow, brought even more to it daily, and moreover, constantly dug or plowed in the field; it is easy to imagine what kind of fruit one could expect to receive from it.

§. 103. In decomposition <term>Verwesung</term> <note>The natural process of the body breaking down after death.</note>, the body will suffer neither torment nor loss, for it is innocent and insensible, as we can perceive in every lifeless corpse. In this [earthly] time, the body has had to act only as a pure passive recipient <term>patiens</term> <note>original: "patiens"; a philosophical term for the thing being acted upon, rather than the actor.</note>, driven with force by the soul toward either good or evil. All sensation, whether it be good or evil, lies in the soul, just as all effects—good and evil—come from it. To this soul, it will now be a great harm and a pain when it must see all its imagined goods, such as beauty, pleasure, honor, wealth, and glory, perish in the divine fire; it will see itself completely stripped and impoverished before the face of God and all the saints, while seeing others decorated and clothed with glory and splendor from the divine light.

§. 104. The transfiguration <term>Verklärung</term> of our bodies depends <note>original: "dependiret"</note> on nothing else than upon the soul having been anointed or tinctured <term>tingirten</term> <note>original: "tingirten"; a term likely borrowed from alchemy, meaning to infuse or permeate with a specific quality or essence.</note> in this time by Jehovah, or by the Spirit of Christ. §. 105. Our current natural body is, by way of comparison, like *congealed* <note>original: "congelirt"; frozen or solidified.</note> or frozen water: as long as the frost is inside it, nothing can penetrate it, unless the hole is as large as the piece of ice or the body itself. But when the frost is gone, and the water is dissolved by heat, it has become *subtle* <note>original: "subtil"; in early modern science and alchemy, this describes a substance so refined and thin that it can pass through solid matter.</note> and can penetrate all <term>pores</term> <note>original: "Poros"; tiny openings or passages in a material.</note>. It is just the same with our bodies: when the *coagulation* <note>original: "Coagulation"; the process of a liquid becoming solid; here, it refers to the density of the physical body.</note> is gone, they have become *subtle*, like the spirits, and like Christ after his resurrection, able to go through closed doors and to *penetrate* everything. And just as the whole piece of ice is <term>dissolved</term> <note>original: "solviret"; to be broken down into a liquid or spiritual form.</note> and <term>transmuted</term> <note>original: "transmutiret"; transformed from one state of being into another.</note> into water by heat, so too our entire natural body is dissolved by the <note>The "cold fire" refers to a spiritual or alchemical heat that transforms matter into spirit without physical combustion.</note> cold fire and made spiritual, so that the whole human <term>mechanism</term> <note>original: "Machine"; a common 17th-18th century term for the biological body as a functional system.</note>, according to the greater or lesser degree of light (1 Corinthians 15:40–41), enters into a new body. Therefore, one should not treat the bones of the dead as if there were nothing good or useful left in them, for they are the very thing from which the spiritual body is to be formed (chapter 8:37).

§. 106. Just as this *decay* <note>original: "Putrefaction"; in alchemy, the stage where matter rots and turns black before being purified.</note> and *dissolution* <note>original: "Solutio"; the breaking down of a solid substance.</note> do not happen *in a single moment* <note>original: "uno momento"</note> or all at once—as mentioned in 1 Corinthians 15:52—but rather happen gradually and through a *continuous process* <note>original: "actu continuo"</note> because the central fire does not yet have full power, so too the resurrection and reunion of the soul and the body happen through a *continuous process*, 1 Thessalonians 4:15. However much of the deceased body is *dissolved* <note>original: "solvirt"</note> daily or yearly, that same amount is simultaneously *transmuted* <note>original: "transmutiret"; transformed from a base state to a higher, spiritual one</note> and reunited with the departed soul. This *continues* <note>original: "continuiret"</note> until the natural body has completely rotted and dissolved, and the soul is washed and purified of its *imprinted* <note>original: "imprimirten"; stamped or fixed upon the soul</note> false *ideas*. At the end, nothing remains of the entire human body—once it has lain for its allotted time in corruption—except for a tiny bit of earth that one can hold in one’s hand. Into this [earth], the eternal darkness had an entrance, as if to its own physical child; this earth is also the cause of all sickness, and even death itself.

And when he actually ate, he fell with his body into the realm of mortality, and now dwelt between Heaven and Hell, where death and life, light and darkness, constantly take to the field <note>original: "zu Felde liegen"; a military metaphor for opposing armies camping in the field of battle</note> against one another for the human fortress.

§. III. In this mortal realm we are all conceived and born, where we are as close to Hell as to Heaven. If we do not now become wise through <term>Adam’s</term> fall and through our own fall, so that we learn to understand and flee the serpent's cunning, then we fall one degree deeper than <term>Adam</term> placed us; namely, we fall from the **Third Principle** <term>Third Principle: In this context, the physical, material world where the spiritual forces of light and darkness are mixed together</term> entirely into the realm of Hell with body and soul. That was also <term>Lucifer’s</term> <term>intention</term> and will against <term>Adam</term>. However, during the eating and during the process of becoming mortal, <term>Jehovah</term>, as the eternal light, embodied Himself within the human being. Such embodied and concentrated light was to him as an anchor, so that he could hold fast to **GOD**, and by which also the great <term>Jehovah</term> could take hold of him again, so that he would not fall entirely into Hell and become like <term>Lucifer</term>. Therefore <term>Moses</term> says,

§. 119. That **Enoch** and **Elijah** <note>original: "Henoch und Helias"; two biblical figures who, according to scripture, were taken up to heaven without dying a natural death.</note> did not taste temporal death at all has no other cause than the great abundance of their spirit. This would have been just as possible for the person of Christ as it was for these two—indeed, even more so, for Christ was conceived and born without sin. How else should the kingdom of death have lost its dominion over those bodies which have little spirit, and are thus very tightly bound with the chains of death? Therefore, Christ went into death for the sake of sinners, and not for the sake of the righteous. Furthermore, neither Enoch nor Elijah caused any damage to the kingdom of death and hell outside of themselves; Christ alone did this.

§. 121. In this corrupted bodily constitution now, Adam fathered children of his own kind, and thus bequeathed death to all his descendants, 1 Corinthians 15:21. By virtue of the **dust** <note>Referring to the biblical account in Genesis 2:7, where man is formed from the dust of the ground.</note> created within him, Genesis 2:7, the desire came upon him. Had he not allowed himself to lust after eating the forbidden fruit of Good and Evil, but had instead paid attention to the **divine light** <note>A common concept in Pietist and mystical thought, referring to the internal presence of God's wisdom or the soul's conscience.</note> shining within him, and tested all occurrences according to that same light, then the desire would have been stifled and would never have reached its **fruition** <note>original: "Ausgebuhrt"; literally an "out-birthing" or the physical manifestation of an internal thought or urge.</note>. Consequently, Adam would have remained free from both sin and death. However, because he acted contrary to the Spirit of God forbidding him in his soul, Genesis 3:3, and nevertheless consented, he committed the sin through that consent, and the actual eating brought him death, James 1:15. Firstly, through the gazing upon the tree, the consent and sin increased, but through the act of eating itself, mortality increased. This mortality is nothing other than an **accidental property** <term>Accidens</term> <note>original: "Accidens"; a philosophical term for a non-essential quality or characteristic that is added to a thing, rather than being part of its core nature.</note> and an excess of dark dust, which excess Adam did not have in Paradise, but first received through eating, and thereby became mortal.

§. 122. Because we are all now begotten from such seeds, in which a strong dose of the dark dust <note>In this context, "dust" refers to the base, material, and mortal elements of human nature derived from the earth, which the author associates with our fallen state.</note> is contained, this very excess of such dust is Original Sin. It acts as a <term>matrix</term> <note>original: "Matrix"; a source, mold, or womb in which something is formed.</note> for actual sin, which is continually propagated among all of Adam’s children through the seed as a <term>potential</term> <note>original: "Potentia"; an inherent capacity or latent power that has not yet been acted upon.</note> and as a seed for sin. As the years of growth pass, it increases through the <term>nourishment</term> <note>original: "Nutriment"; the food and fuel that sustains the physical body.</note> of food and breaks out into reality. For this reason, Christ and his Apostles warn against gluttony and drunkenness; one should use food and drink for necessity, but not for luxury and carnal lust, so that the dust is not <term>aroused</term> <note>original: "excitiret"; stirred up or stimulated into action.</note>. For through their excessive use, our "dust" is increased to such an extent that its powers overflow in words and deeds. Indeed, daily experience teaches that those who attend too much to the body with all kinds of dainty foods and strong drinks commit various gross excesses and sins, which would not happen in a state of sobriety and moderation.

§. 123. Against this dust, we must fight as against our hereditary enemy for as long as we live, and we must <term>beseech</term> <note>original: "imploriren"; to beg or pray for earnestly.</note> the eternal light **JESUS** for help and <term>succor</term> <note>original: "Succurs"; military-style reinforcements or spiritual assistance.</note> from above, so that the eternal darkness does not gain the rule within us and make us children of death and hell. For when **God** the **Lord** formed man from the dust of the earth and breathed into him the **twofold breath** <note>original: "zweyfachen Odem"; likely referring to the combination of the natural life-force and the immortal soul or spirit.</note>, it did not happen so that the

...[the] soul <note>The sentence continues from the previous page, contrasting the soul's original purpose with its fallen state.</note> should [not] conceive the seed of eternal darkness and, by means of the dust <note>As established previously, "dust" refers to our physical, earthly nature.</note>, give birth to it in words and deeds; but she, the soul, was meant to be a bride of eternal light, and through the dust make manifest the mysteries of light. But since he [Adam] enlarged this [dust] by eating the earthly fruit, and passed such enlargement on to us as an inheritance, thereby granting eternal darkness greater entry to himself and us, we must now stand well on guard and constantly struggle against its influences, so that we do not lose our eternal inheritance. But if we show no earnestness in such a struggle, the Devil must only laugh at it, especially when we wage war against the enemies of others and not our own, and want to convert people only with words but not with our conduct—that is, when we only speak finely about it, even *recommending* the matter to others in the best way, but do not wish to take it up and *practice* <note>original: "practiciren"; to live out or apply these spiritual truths in one's own life.</note> it ourselves. In such a case, the Devil thinks: *I and the chatterbox have established an understanding with each other; although he scolds me, it is not meant so maliciously, and because he himself does not practice what he says, he grants me such an advantage that I gain many into my kingdom and as my subjects who would otherwise escape me.*

§. 124. Therefore, we must pay close attention to the <term>original sin</term> <note>original: "Erb-Sünde"; literally "inherited sin," the innate fallen state of humanity.</note> inherent in us all, so that we may prevent its activity and its outbreaks through the help of the divine light. This is so that, after our death, the divine fire may not find so much to consume in our works and in our soul, lest we must suffer great loss. 1 Corinthians 3:15. <note>The author references the biblical idea of one's life's work being tested by fire; if the work is burned, the person is saved "only as through fire."</note>

§. 125. However, although in this life we often **gain victory** <note>original: "victorisiren"</note> over our internal passions, lusts, and desires dwelling in our <term>dust</term> <note>As established previously, "dust" refers to the physical, earthly body and its base impulses.</note>, we cannot yet completely **triumph** <note>original: "triumphiren"</note> over them until the conflict is at an end. As long as we still stand in the struggle, we may well speak of **victory**, but of no **triumph**, for original sin—the overcoming of which shall one day truly grant the triumph—still stirs within us and wishes to **act**. <note>original: "agiren"</note> 2 Corinthians 12:7. This causes us a constant, renewed struggle, and this lasts until the very end of our lives.

§. 126. When the soul, as our **agent** <note>original: "Agens"; the active, driving force of the human being.</note>, is separated from the body, then the activity of the dust ceases; for the soul is, as it were, a double channel through which either the eternal light or the eternal darkness enters into us and accomplishes its work through the body. Positioned above this double channel is the will, which must always provide the deciding factor. Whichever of the two eternal **principles** <note>original: "Principiis"; refers to the fundamental forces of Light/God and Darkness/Evil.</note> the will opens itself to, that one enters and operates. In this, the effects of the eternal light are pure <term>blessedness</term> <note>original: "Seligkeit"; a state of spiritual joy and salvation.</note>, while the effects of the eternal dark-

§. 127. Therefore, they were also led to establish a purification of souls or a <term>purgatory</term> <note>original: "Fege-Feuer"</note> after death. §. 128. If both were used *properly* <note>original: "debite"</note>, they would be of very great benefit.

<margin>Redemption</margin>
§. 138. Insofar as this is the case (namely, regarding the <term>redemption; Latin: redemtio</term> of our body from the realm of the dead), the <term>Satisfaction of Christ; Latin: satisfactio Christi, the theological doctrine that Christ’s suffering paid the debt for human sin</term> also has its certain and truthful foundation. It is no empty imagination, as some who do not look deeply enough into this mystery would believe; rather, as I have clearly and according to the Scriptures shown, it is something real, and according to the body, it benefits all people. However, some might come to the thought that I intend to make Christ into a sinner by attributing <term>original sin; the state of sin believed to be inherited by all humans from Adam</term> to him. To those I wish to briefly respond by saying: I intend to make him neither a sinner nor a servant of sin. Instead, I appeal to the First Epistle of John, which says expressly that *having* sin does not make one a sinner, but *doing* sin makes one a sinner; likewise, that when one is born of God, they cannot actually sin.

§. 139. I know well enough that this proposition will appear very paradoxical to many, as indeed it is; however, I assure the reader that if they will set aside all <term>prejudices of authority; Latin: præjudicia auctoritatis, the tendency to believe something just because an official source says so</term> and, alongside the Holy Scriptures, use—NB. <note>short for the Latin "nota bene," meaning "take special note"</note>—sound reason, they will clearly see from the reasons cited here and the referenced <term>biblical sayings; Latin: dictis biblicis</term> that I have written the truth. I do not at all infringe upon the merit of Christ, but only wish to see it cleansed from misunderstanding and abuse. For some would—

then indeed, people—who are already naturally more inclined toward evil than toward good—must necessarily arrive at these thoughts, especially when those teaching do not struggle against their own passions, lusts, and desires: 1) If the struggle against sin meant so much, the leader would surely do it himself. 2) Even if they sinned greatly, Christ had died for their sins, and for that reason, God would one day grant them grace again. §. 141. This statement by Christ Himself clearly testifies that the <term>Satisfaction; Latin: satisfactio, the doctrine that Christ’s death paid the legal debt for human sin</term> cannot extend to actual sins <note>In this context, "actual sins" refers to specific, individual acts of wrongdoing committed after one's conversion, as opposed to inherited "original sin"</note>. §. 143. I have now shown through <term>reasons; Latin: rationes</term> and through the <term>Scripture; German: Schrifft</term> the main purpose of **God**, why He had to become man, suffer, die, and rise again: namely, for the sake of the resurrection of our bodies, and so that the <term>essence; Latin: essentia</term> from which our body is made (**Acts 17:28, 1 Corinthians 6:20**) might not remain eternally in death and in the kingdom of darkness.

§. 144. §. 145. <margin>
Concerning the union of the soul with the body <note>original: "de unione animae cum corpore"</note>
</margin>

[The body] could do evil if it were not driven to it by force through the soul; we see this in every deceased person, who no longer moves, and because the inhabitant <note>The "inhabitant" is a metaphor for the soul residing within the body</note> is gone, does neither good nor evil anymore. Therefore, the body also has the **benefit of Christ’s satisfaction** <note>original: "beneficium satisfactionis Christi"; the theological doctrine that Christ's sacrifice paid the debt for human sin</note> for itself, so that after the soul's departure, it rests quite gently without any **torment** <note>original: "Quaal"</note> or sensation in its mother the earth—from which it grew—until its complete **dissolution** <note>original: "Solution"; referring here to the natural breaking down of the physical form into its elements</note>. The soul, however, in which actual sins **magically** <term>original: "magisch"; in this mystical context, "magically" refers to an internal, spiritual reality or a force of will rather than sorcery</term> dwell, must either entirely deny these sins during this time [on earth], following the guidance of Scripture, and constantly and earnestly fight and strive against them in the spirit and through the Spirit of Christ, forcing them under the Spirit’s dominion to such an extent that they can no longer break out in **passions** <note>original: "Affecten"; strong emotions or uncontrolled urges</note>, words, or deeds; or, upon departure from this body, the soul takes these sins **magically** with it into the next world. There, in the many and varied mansions of the Heavenly Father—**John 14:2**—a room best suited for it will be assigned, where it must atone for all actual sins and remain still in such mansions until it learns to thoroughly recognize and hate the abominable nature of sin. It must also be completely purified and **magically** washed from sin by the Spirit of Christ; this process proceeds there through many ages <note>original: "Ewigkeiten"; literally "eternities," here likely referring to long cycles of time or "aeons"</note> in a tedious and painful manner, whereas one could have escaped it briefly and easily through the denial of sins in this life.

might heal again the damage caused by Adam, as I have sufficiently proven above with many arguments and reasons <note>original: "Argumentis und Rationibus"</note>, and I add in conclusion to this little work: that Christ, the Savior of the whole world, had to make the beginning in himself for the overcoming <term>Überwindung: A central Pietist concept referring to the spiritual victory over one's own lower nature and worldly temptations</term> of sin, death, the devil, and hell; for how else could he have pointed us toward the following and overcoming of such mighty enemies, if he himself had not gone before and overcome them therein? Revelation <note>original: "Apoc."</note> 3:21, Luke <note>original: "Luc."</note> 9:23. And how could he, as a true human, know that such overcoming is so difficult, and thus have compassion for our weakness, if he did not have it from practice and experience <note>original: "Praxi und Erfahrung"</note>? Therefore, he now gives us of his Spirit and helps our weakness, so that we also can overcome, just as he has overcome. And since we wish to be called followers and members of his body, we must not let our Head [Christ] work and struggle alone, but we must also do our part, and we must always make the beginning of overcoming within ourselves and not in others.

§. 159. The principle <note>original: "Principium"</note> of death, into which eternal darkness has an entrance, caused the Savior many a struggle and many a temptation, internally and externally; but he always overcame, constantly crushing the head of the serpent within himself, and never consented to temptations, much less allowed them to break out. §. 160. §. 162. §. 164. Perhaps by this I might induce one or another to turn from the common medical rut to the true Universal Medicine, and from chemical sophistry to the knowledge of the true Tincture. The sign is this: A Universal Medicine and Tincture must consist of nothing but pure, concentrated, fixed, and exalted particles of life and light, which are of the same essence and nature as the natural life of man. For because life is nothing other than a light that depends chiefly upon the sun; and diseases, on the other hand, are nothing other than a **partial deprivation or suppression** <note>original: "Particularis privatio vel suppressio"</note> of this natural life and light in the human body; thus the cure for diseases is nothing other than the replacement of such deprived particles of life through a righteous medicine. ...[substan]tial little branch of our **Mercury** <note>original: "Mercurii"</note> and shows, as can be seen in the lead mines, that no lead ore is found from which a permanent grain of gold or silver could not be extracted; for the first binding or freezing of **Mercury** <note>original: "Mercurii"</note> is an **ore of Saturn** <note>original: "Minera Saturni"; Saturn is the alchemical name for lead</note>, or a lead ore, which truly can be brought to its **perfection** <note>original: "Perfection"</note>, but the **fixed grain** <note>original: "Körnlein Fixi"</note> must still remain within the **Mercury** <note>original: "Mercurio"</note>, and by no means be separated from its ore or lead ore, otherwise it is brought out by the power of the fire and turned into silver, which is of no use. For when a metal still lies in its ore or workshop, it is a **Mercury** <note>original: "Mercurius"</note>, from which if its fixed grain is separated, it is just as if one picks an unripe apple from the tree, which completely spoils and cannot become perfect.

§. 179. p. 50. We should and must take the very materials from which Nature makes the metals in the earth. **Bernhard** <note>Likely referring to Bernard of Trevisan, a famous 15th-century alchemist who argued that the "Stone" must be made from metallic substances.</note> says that Nature first makes a leaden **matter** <note>original: "Materiam"</note>, in which she has placed her first **disposition** <note>original: "Disposition"</note> and property; there one can also come closest to it. The other bodies, such as **Gold and Silver** <note>original: "Sol & Luna"</note> etc., are too hard to unlock; therefore we call them a **body** <note>original: "Corpus"</note>, in which there is exactly such a pure **sulfur** <note>original: "Sulphur"</note> and **mercury** <note>original: "Mercurius"</note> as in silver and gold, in which Nature has only worked a little, but left it imperfect. Page 52. In our lead, before it touches the fire, there is **potentially** <note>original: "potentia"</note> gold and silver, even if one cannot see it when it is together. But when its spirit separates from the body, it leaves the **lunar body** <note>original: "Corpus lunare"; referring to the substance of silver</note> behind, like the most beautiful silver in all tests. Page 53. The **Assembly of Philosophers** <note>original: "Turba"; referring to the *Turba Philosophorum*, one of the most ancient and influential alchemical texts.</note> says: that in **Saturn** <note>original: "Saturno"; lead</note>, which has not yet been in the fire, there is always a grain or several of **Gold or Silver** <note>original: "Solis oder Lunae"</note> hidden, like a seed that can be propagated. Therefore the **Rose Garden** <note>original: "Rosarius"; likely the *Rosarium Philosophorum*, an important 16th-century alchemical treatise.</note> says: Our **Gold or Silver** <note>original: "Sol oder Luna"</note>, or the **fixed body** <note>original: "corpus fixum"</note> is hidden, just like the soul in the human body. See, I have shown you the opportunity of our white lead (that is, when it is brought into a white salt through **resolution** <term>Resolution: In this context, the process of dissolving or breaking a solid down into its essential liquid or salt state.</term>). When you know that, the rest is **women's work and children's play** <note>A common alchemical idiom meaning that once the correct substance is prepared, the final stages of the process require only gentle heat and patience rather than strenuous labor.</note>. He wishes to imply that after the **composition** <note>original: "Composition"</note> it is the easiest work of all.

<meta>catchword: §. 180.</meta>

<summary>This page explains that lead (Saturn) is the primary material for alchemy because it contains the "seeds" of gold and silver in an unfinished state. It cites classical authorities to argue that if lead is processed correctly without being "killed" by harsh fire, it can be perfected into precious metals.</summary>
