§. 180. p. 58. **Lullius** <note>Raymond Lully (c. 1232–1315), a philosopher and alchemist.</note> and **Matthesius** <note>Johannes Mathesius (1504–1565), a German minister and mineralogist known for his "Sermons on Mines."</note> write: that the **matter** <note>original: "Materia"</note> of metals, before they solidify into a metallic form, should be like a buttermilk and can be smeared like butter; this he calls **Gur** <note>A German mining term for a soft, fatty mineral substance found in rock fissures, believed by alchemists to be "unripe" metal.</note>, just as I myself have found such in mines where nature has produced lead. And if one can also produce such a **matter** <note>original: "Materiam"</note> up here on the earth, it shall be a certain sign that one not only has the right **matter** <note>original: "Materiam"</note>, but is undoubtedly on the right path; I can, praise be to God, easily make this in my hand, etc. This the philosophers call **Virgin's Milk** <note>original: "Lac virginis"</note>. If one puts a little **metallic salt** <note>original: "Salis metallici"</note> into our water, it becomes like a white milk; but if one puts much into it, it becomes thick like butter and can be smeared like fat or the like. Our **matter** <note>original: "Materia"</note> is commonly called **Magnesia**, which means in the **Chaldean** <note>A term often used historically for Aramaic or ancient Babylonian; here the author provides a mystical etymology.</note> language: "unmoved lead," which is to say, it is still exactly as nature **prepared** <note>original: "præpariret"</note> it, and has not yet passed through human hands, much less encountered the fire.

§. 181. p. 59. All **metals** <note>original: "Metalla"</note>, the pure as well as the impure, are inwardly **Gold, Silver, and Mercury** <note>original: "Sol, Luna u. Mercurius"</note>. It is well worth considering that a metallic and **Saturnine body** <note>original: "saturnisch Corpus"; Saturn is the alchemical symbol for lead.</note> exists which one can easily **dissolve** <note>original: "solviren"</note> and **purify** <note>original: "purificiren"</note>; whoever knows its right **preparation** <note>original: "Præparation"</note> may rightly rejoice. **Turba** <note>Referring to the *Turba Philosophorum* (Assembly of the Philosophers), one of the oldest Latin alchemical texts.</note>: I tell you that our sulfurous living water is extracted from lead ore, which accomplishes everything. Likewise: You should know that the spirit of silver and all whiteness must be composed from lead ore in such a way that it becomes **Silver** <note>original: "Luna"</note>, a white stone (**salt of metals** <note>original: "sal metallorum"</note>), without any blemish or impurity. The whiteness that shines there is drawn from the **Saturnine body** <note>original: "saturnischē Corpore"</note>; it is beautiful and clear, and one sees no **Silver** <note>original: "Lunam"</note> in it, but if one throws it onto a hot **Copper** <note>original: "Veneris"</note> or **Iron** <note>original: "Martis"</note> plate, it melts like wax, and the **spirit** <note>original: "Spiritus"</note> of life flies away above it, leaving the **Moon-plant** <note>original: "Lunariam"; likely referring here to the silver precipitate.</note> lying beautifully behind. For the **white Silver** <note>original: "alba Luna"</note> is not to be looked upon like common silver, for it is transparent. Page 60. **Theophrastus’s** <note>Theophrastus Paracelsus (1493–1541), the famous Swiss-German physician and alchemist.</note> **unripe mineral electrum** <note>original: "electum minerale immaturum"</note> is such a thing, in which nature has planted the properties of all seven metals but left them incomplete; therefore he calls it: **unripe** <note>original: "immaturum"</note>.

Villach <note>A city in southern Austria famous for its lead mines.</note>, a very solid lead that has not been through the fire, which is easily dissolved, frequently has its **Lunaria** <note>original: "Lunariam"; likely referring to a silver-bearing essence or "moon-power" within the ore.</note> with it, and is marked thus: <image-desc>alchemical symbol for Saturn/Lead with a horizontal cross-bar through the vertical stroke</image-desc>; or that which is even better is found in Meissen <note>A town in Saxony, Germany.</note>, though it is rarely found at this time, yet if one orders it, one can obtain it. And there they call it **Glass-Ore** <term>Glaß-Ertz: Argentite, a soft, lead-grey silver ore that can be cut with a knife.</term>; it is like lead, easy to cut and emboss, and a hundredweight holds well 24 to 26 **lot** <note>A "lot" was a traditional German unit of weight, roughly equivalent to 1/32nd of a pound.</note> of silver, from which I have obtained well 22 lot of **Mercury** <note>original: "Mercurii"</note> out of one pound. There is also Glass-Ore at St. Annaberg <note>A famous mining town in the Ore Mountains of Saxony.</note>, which is also rich in silver, indistinguishable from other lead, also allows itself to be embossed and cut, and is good to dissolve.

## From the Little Peasant.
<note>Refers to "Der Kleine Bauer," a well-known alchemical treatise often attributed to Johann Grasshoff.</note>

§. 183. Page 111. We know that in all of nature there is no thing more closely related to the human body, or which has a greater **correspondence** <note>original: "Convenientiam"</note> with the same, than the metals themselves, and especially the purest ones, such as gold and silver; this is shown by their beautiful luster and constancy. From this it is easy to conclude that—**Note Well**—their spirit or **tincture** <note>original: "Tinctur"; the transformative essence or soul of a substance.</note> (which is enclosed within all metals, and is much closer to be obtained in lead than in gold) possesses such constancy and virtue within itself and acts upon other things. Therefore, these two noble metals, which are by nature equal to the human body, can—for whoever knows how to prepare and use them rightly—infuse such high health that they alone can be the **threefold point of the Universal Way** <note>The "Universal Way" refers to the pursuit of the Philosopher's Stone, which was believed to heal all things.</note>, and **plant matter** <note>original: "Vegetabilia"</note> is not to be compared to metals even by 1,000 degrees. When these two flowers <note>Metaphorical names for Gold and Silver.</note> have been brought to their inner purity through the right natural and proper **philosophical preparation**, they are entirely comparable to the heavenly stars, such as the Sun and Moon, which illuminate day and night, the upper and lower firmament, with their clarity. But an inexperienced person might object and ask you: from whence it comes that the metals have such **sympathy** <note>original: "Sympathiam"</note>, correspondence, love, and friendship with humans, animals, and herbs, since flesh and bone and metals and minerals stand as far apart from each other as heaven and earth in his eyes, and **differ**—

<summary>This page describes high-quality lead and silver ores from Villach and Meissen before transitioning into an alchemical defense of metallic medicine, arguing that gold and silver possess a unique spiritual sympathy with the human body.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the discussion of the "correspondence" between the human body and metals, refuting the idea that humanity originates from base dirt, and begins a detailed alchemical analogy between the biblical Creation and the "Great Work" of alchemy.</meta>

<page-num>111</page-num>

---

pire? <note>Continuing from the previous page: "...and expire?"</note> But such a notion is easily refuted if one first considers the original generation of humans and metals and holds them against one another. Man was not created by **God** the Almighty out of a poor, impure, or common clod of earth—as unlearned theologians and loud-mouths <note>original: "Clamanten"; referring to those who shout without knowledge.</note> believe—but rather from the very best, most subtle extract that existed *in the entire center of the earth* <note>original: "in toto centro terræ"</note>. Into this, He also set, breathed, and implanted a spark or ray of His eternal essence and being. He did not take common earth, but as mentioned, took the *extract of the whole* <note>original: "extractum totius"</note>. This is indeed found to be true when a human is resolved and brought back into his *three principles* <note>original: "tria principia"; in alchemy, these are Salt, Sulfur, and Mercury, the fundamental components of all matter.</note>; for then, at the end of the composition, a beautiful, glistening red earth reveals itself.

§. 184. page 189. In short, the entire **Work of the Philosophers** <note>original: "Opus Philosophorum"; the "Great Work" of creating the Philosopher's Stone.</note> is nothing other than making new heavens and earths: drawing the heaven down, and swinging the earth above itself, raising and setting it on high in place of the heaven. This proceeds exactly as **God** the **Lord** acted in the creation of the world. For in the beginning, when **God** the **Lord** created heaven and earth, it was all merely a water, which was the *first matter* <note>original: "prima Materia"</note>. Within this water, heaven and earth with their entire host, together with the abyss of the earth—which was encompassed with darkness, empty, and submerged without power or life—were contained. Over the water, as the purest and best part, hovered the Spirit of **God**, which is a consuming fire and life, and preserved the same. But the abyss of the earth and the darkness, as the useless, dead part, He abandoned; He drew only the powers of light and life out of the earth into the water, so that right at the beginning, through the Spirit of **God**, a separation occurred within this solution and putrefaction <term>Putrefaction: a stage in alchemy where matter is broken down or "rotted" to be purified and reborn.</term>. Since the single essence and life of all creatures was contained in this solution, the Spirit of **God** rightly cherished, covered, strengthened, and ripened it for the increase of His perfect light of life, just as a hen protects her chicks with the wings of her almighty fertility. Now, as through this solution and

<meta>This page concludes the alchemical analogy of the biblical Creation, explaining how the "waters" of the universe are separated and reunited, and how the alchemist (the Artist) must follow this divine pattern to achieve the Great Work.</meta>

<page-num>112</page-num>
<header>❀ ( 0 ) ❀</header>

---

<term>Putrefaction</term> <note>The stage of decay or fermentation; alchemists believed matter must "die" and rot before it could be reborn in a purified state.</note> everything hastened toward its actual state of life, there followed the right, essential <term>Separation</term> <note>The process of dividing the pure from the impure, or the spirit from the body.</note> of the uppermost, middlemost, and lowermost waters, so that **GOD** might thereafter bring forth various creatures from their mixing, according to the weight of nature and according to the opportunity of the places. Through the middle water, the uppermost water gives life and increase to all lower creatures created from the physical water. Thus far says the Farmer <note>likely referring to a specific alchemical author known as "The Peasant" or "The Farmer."</note>.

§. 185. The uppermost and middlemost waters stand now in constant <term>Sublimation</term> <note>The process where a substance is heated into vapor and then condensed; here, it refers to the ongoing spiritual refinement of the universe.</note>, until the lower physical waters—presently lying under the curse and bound with chains of darkness—are once again set loose and free through the general burning away of the curse. Then these physical waters, by virtue of their great magnetic power, will draw all the upper and middle waters back to themselves; then heaven and earth will again become one thing, and the upper waters will <term>tinge</term> <note>original: "tingiren"; to transform, color, or "dye" a substance into a higher state, such as turning base metal into gold.</note>, improve, and give birth to a new crystalline earth, as I have proven at length above.

§. 186. Now, when the Artist <note>The alchemist.</note> follows **GOD** in His creation according to nature with his <term>microcosmic</term> <note>Relating to the "small world" of the alchemist’s vessel, which mirrors the "macrocosm" or the entire universe.</note> work, and has loosed his Magnet from its earthly bonds and turned it into water, and has also reached the essential <term>Separation</term>, he can thereafter no longer fail in the <term>Conjunction</term> <note>The "marriage" or union of the separated elements.</note> and <term>Imbibition</term> <note>The process of "feeding" the dry alchemical material with liquid to nourish its growth.</note> of the upper and middle waters into the lower.

§. 187. Should one or another perhaps receive a light from this small sketch, let him handle it with caution, and give the glory to **GOD** alone.

->THE END.<- <note>original: "FINIS."</note>
